SECTION 17 : DEVELOPMENT WORKFLOW — HOW WE BUILD THIS PROJECT
====================================================
  Last Updated  : 2026-06-12 (Session 28)
  Purpose       : Complete guide to how every session is run — which model,
                  how to design, how to confirm, how to test with Playwright,
                  and how to fix errors. Read this at the start of every session.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
17.1  WHICH AI MODEL TO USE
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  TOOL         : Claude Code (CLI) — run it in VS Code terminal or Windows Terminal
  COMMAND      : claude   (opens interactive session in the project folder)
  WORKING DIR  : C:\Users\DELL\Desktop\keval\Cavero Fragrances

  PRIMARY MODEL (coding, building, debugging):
    claude-sonnet-4-6   ← default / recommended for all coding sessions
    Why: fast, accurate with Angular + Node, handles long files well, best
    value for multi-file edits. Switch with /model inside Claude Code.

  HEAVY MODEL (complex architecture, big redesigns, multi-file refactors):
    claude-opus-4       ← use when Sonnet gives wrong answers repeatedly
    Why: deeper reasoning for architectural decisions. Slower + costs more.
    Switch with:  /model claude-opus-4-8

  FAST ITERATION MODEL (quick one-liner fixes, CSS tweaks, renaming):
    claude-haiku-4-5    ← use for trivial changes only
    Switch with:  /model claude-haiku-4-5-20251001

  HOW TO CHECK CURRENT MODEL:
    Type /model  inside Claude Code → shows current + lets you switch.

  SESSION 28 MODEL USED: claude-sonnet-4-6 (default)
    All homepage redesign (7 component rewrites + new sections) was done
    on Sonnet 4.6 — zero escalation to Opus needed.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
17.2  HOW WE DESIGN CHANGES (THE DESIGN FLOW)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  STEP 1 — PROVIDE REFERENCE
    Give Claude a screenshot, URL, or text description of what you want.
    Example: "Make the homepage look like this [screenshot]"
    The more specific the better — include which sections, colors, layout.

  STEP 2 — CLAUDE ASKS CLARIFYING QUESTIONS (AskUserQuestion tool)
    Claude will ask 1-4 multiple-choice questions before touching any code.
    These lock in decisions so you don't get a half-wrong rebuild.
    Typical questions: color scheme? scope (which pages)? approach?
    Always answer these before work starts.

  STEP 3 — CLAUDE CREATES A TASK LIST
    After your answers Claude creates numbered tasks (TaskCreate tool).
    You can see them at the top of the conversation as a progress checklist.
    Each task maps to one component or section — lets you track exactly
    what has and hasn't been touched.

  STEP 4 — COMPONENT-BY-COMPONENT EDITS
    Claude edits one component at a time in this order:
      a. Read the existing file (always reads before any edit)
      b. Write/Edit the .html template
      c. Write/Edit the .scss styles
      d. Write/Edit the .ts logic (only if needed)
    Rule: Claude NEVER writes a file without reading it first.

  STEP 5 — BUILD CHECK
    After all components are done: npm run build
    Zero TypeScript errors = safe to proceed.
    Pre-existing warnings (CommonJS, budget) are fine — they were there before.

  STEP 6 — VISUAL CONFIRM (see 17.3)

  DESIGN RULES WE FOLLOW:
    · Light pages: cream bg #FFF8E7, white section cards, gold #C9A84C accents
    · Dark pages: base #0D0B07, surface #1A1510, cream text #FFF8E7, gold #C9A84C
    · Font headings: Playfair Display (italic/700 for hero, 700 for titles)
    · Font body:     DM Sans (600 for labels/buttons, 400/500 for text)
    · Button radius: 6px (pills for nav), 14px (cards), 20px (section cards)
    · Homepage: light editorial (Session 28+)
    · All other pages (catalog, cart, checkout, product, auth, admin): dark gold

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
17.3  HOW TO CONFIRM VISUALLY (SCREENSHOT FLOW)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  THE PROCESS:
    1. App must be running:   npm run dev
    2. Claude writes a temp Node.js Playwright script → saves to tests/screenshot-home.js
    3. Claude runs:           node tests/screenshot-home.js
    4. Screenshots saved to:  tests/home-full.png  +  tests/home-viewport.png
    5. Claude reads the PNG (multimodal) → reports what it sees
    6. If a section is blank: scroll-trigger is added (appAnimateOnScroll makes
       sections opacity:0 until scrolled into — headless screenshots need JS scroll)
    7. Claude deletes all temp screenshot files after confirming
       (they are not committed to git)

  SCRIPT PATTERN (what Claude generates):
    const { chromium } = require('@playwright/test');
    (async () => {
      const browser = await chromium.launch();
      const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: { width: 1440, height: 900 } });
      await page.goto('http://localhost:4200/', { waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' });
      await page.waitForTimeout(8000);         // let API + images load
      // scroll entire page to fire AnimateOnScroll
      await page.evaluate(async () => {
        for (let y = 0; y <= document.body.scrollHeight; y += 600) {
          window.scrollTo(0, y);
          await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 250));
        }
      });
      await page.waitForTimeout(1500);
      await page.screenshot({ path: 'tests/home-full.png', fullPage: true });
      await browser.close();
    })();

  PER-SECTION SCREENSHOT (for checking a specific component):
    const el = page.locator('.your-section-class').first();
    await el.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded();
    await page.waitForTimeout(600);
    await el.screenshot({ path: 'tests/sec-name.png' });

  COMMON SCREENSHOT ISSUE — BLANK/TRANSPARENT SECTIONS:
    Cause:  appAnimateOnScroll starts opacity:0 — IntersectionObserver fires
            only when element enters viewport. Headless browser starts at top.
    Fix:    Add the JS scroll loop (shown above) before taking the screenshot.
    Verify: Check opacity via page.evaluate() → getComputedStyle(el).opacity

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
17.4  PLAYWRIGHT TEST SUITE — HOW TO RUN & CONFIRM
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  LOCATION: tests/specs/01-homepage.spec.ts  through  09-mobile.spec.ts
  TOTAL   : 67 tests (9 spec files × browsers — Chrome, Firefox, Edge, iPhone 12)
  CONFIG  : playwright.config.ts
  PREREQ  : App must be running (npm run dev) before running tests

  COMMANDS:
    npm test                  → all 67 tests, all 4 browsers (Chrome/Firefox/Edge/iPhone)
    npm run test:chrome       → Chrome only (fastest, use for quick confirm)
    npm run test:mobile       → iPhone 12 390px viewport only
    npm run test:headed       → visible browser windows (debug what's on screen)
    npm run test:report       → open HTML report of last run
    npx playwright test tests/specs/01-homepage.spec.ts --project=chrome
                              → single spec, Chrome only (fastest single-file check)

  WHAT EACH SPEC TESTS:
    01-homepage.spec.ts  — title, logo, 4+ category links, GA4 tag, favicon, og:site_name
    02-signup.spec.ts    — form fields, referral, validation, duplicate email block
    03-signin.spec.ts    — wrong creds error, valid login, JWT in localStorage
    04-catalog.spec.ts   — 10 products, EDP filter, tags, clear-all, category route
    05-product-detail.spec.ts — name, price, badge, add-to-cart
    06-cart.spec.ts      — inject cart, gift bar, thresholds, checkout button
    07-checkout.spec.ts  — phone/ZIP validators, COD E2E, Razorpay mock
    08-admin.spec.ts     — admin login, wrong creds, auth guard, free gifts page
    09-mobile.spec.ts    — 390px iPhone 12, hamburger, all key pages

  AFTER EVERY SESSION: run npm run test:chrome (fastest full sanity check)
  BEFORE DEPLOY: run npm test (all 4 browsers — ~5 minutes)

  WHAT "PASSING" MEANS:
    ✓ All 67 tests green = safe to deploy
    Any red test = regression introduced this session — fix before moving on.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
17.5  HOW TO FIX ERRORS
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ── TYPE 1: Angular Build Errors (TypeScript / compile) ──

    Run:  npm run build  →  look at the ERROR lines, NOT the warnings.
    Show Claude the full error block (copy from terminal or Claude reads it).

    COMMON ERRORS + FIXES:
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Error                           │ Cause                  │ Fix              │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Property does not exist on type │ Added prop to HTML but  │ Add to TS class  │
    │                                 │ not to .ts class        │ or interface     │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Cannot find name 'X'            │ Missing import or typo  │ Add import or    │
    │                                 │                         │ fix spelling     │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Module not found: X             │ Import path wrong or    │ Fix path or      │
    │                                 │ package not installed   │ npm install X    │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Budget exceeded (X kb > Y kb)   │ Component stylesheet    │ Raise budget in  │
    │                                 │ too large               │ angular.json     │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ NG8001/NG8002: Unknown element  │ Component not imported  │ Add to imports[] │
    │ or directive                    │ in standalone component │ in @Component    │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHICH MODEL TO USE FOR BUILD ERRORS:
    → Sonnet 4.6 handles 95% of build errors.
      Paste the full error into Claude, say "fix this build error."
      Claude will read the affected file, identify the mismatch, edit it.

  ── TYPE 2: Runtime Errors (browser console, API failures) ──

    How to see them:
      Browser → F12 → Console tab  (red = JS errors, yellow = warnings)
      OR: Claude writes a temp Playwright debug script that captures console errors.

    COMMON RUNTIME ERRORS + FIXES:
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Error                           │ Cause                  │ Fix              │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ 404 Not Found (image/asset)     │ Wrong image path —     │ Use backend URL: │
    │ assets/uploads/...              │ uploads/ excluded from │ apiGatewayBase + │
    │                                 │ Angular bundle         │ '/uploads/...'   │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ 401 Unauthorized (API call)     │ JWT expired or missing │ Re-login as admin│
    │                                 │                        │ or customer      │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ 403 Forbidden (API call)        │ Route needs admin role │ Check middleware  │
    │                                 │ but no token sent      │ in backend route  │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ CORS error                      │ Backend CORS_ORIGINS   │ Add frontend URL  │
    │                                 │ not set for prod URL   │ to .env CORS_OR.. │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ ExpressionChangedAfterChecked   │ Angular CD timing —    │ Use setTimeout    │
    │                                 │ value changed in AfterV│ or ChangeDetector │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Cannot read property of null    │ API returned null or   │ Add *ngIf guard   │
    │                                 │ component loaded too   │ or ?. optional    │
    │                                 │ fast before API done   │ chain in template │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHICH MODEL TO USE FOR RUNTIME ERRORS:
    → Sonnet 4.6 for most runtime issues — tell Claude the exact error text.
    → Opus 4.8 only if Sonnet gives a wrong fix 2+ times.

  ── TYPE 3: Playwright Test Failures ──

    Run:  npx playwright test tests/specs/XX.spec.ts --project=chrome
    Then: npm run test:report  → visual HTML report with screenshots of failures

    COMMON TEST FAILURES + FIXES:
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Failure type                    │ Cause                  │ Fix              │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Locator timeout (element not    │ Selector changed in    │ Update selector  │
    │ found after 30s)                │ HTML after redesign    │ in spec file     │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Expected X, received Y          │ Text/count changed     │ Update expected  │
    │ (assertion mismatch)            │ (e.g. category count   │ value in spec    │
    │                                 │ went from 7→4)         │                  │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Test passes locally but fails   │ Missing browser binary │ npx playwright   │
    │ on fresh machine                │                        │ install          │
    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ Flaky test (passes sometimes)   │ Race condition — API   │ Add waitForLoad  │
    │                                 │ slower than expected   │ or increase      │
    │                                 │                        │ timeout          │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    HOW TO GET CLAUDE TO FIX A TEST FAILURE:
      1. Run:  npx playwright test tests/specs/XX.spec.ts --project=chrome 2>&1
      2. Copy the FAILED line + "Error:" block
      3. Tell Claude: "This Playwright test is failing, fix it: [paste error]"
      4. Claude reads the spec file and the component, identifies the mismatch,
         updates EITHER the spec selector OR the component HTML (whichever is wrong).

  ── TYPE 4: Backend / API Errors ──

    See backend logs in the terminal running npm run dev (yellow BACKEND: lines).
    Common issues:
      · "EADDRINUSE :::5000" → backend already running; kill it (Ctrl+C) and restart
      · "relation does not exist" → new table not migrated; node backend/auth-service/migrate.js
      · "invalid token" → JWT_SECRET changed; users must re-login
      · "Cannot read ... of undefined" → API response shape changed; update frontend

    WHICH MODEL TO USE: Sonnet 4.6 for backend fixes too.
    Tell Claude the exact terminal error + which route/file is involved.

====================================================
  SESSION 46 — CLOUDINARY IMAGE PERSISTENCE (DEPLOY BLOCKER) ✅ DONE
  Last Updated : 2026-06-28
====================================================

  WHY: cloud hosts have an EPHEMERAL local disk — anything written to
  /uploads is wiped on every redeploy/restart. All admin-uploaded media
  (products, categories, sliders, reels, logo, brands, combos, combo-box,
  about) must live on Cloudinary or it vanishes after deploy. This was the
  single remaining hard blocker before going live.

  WHAT WAS DONE
    - config/cloudinary.js — central uploader. Reads CLOUDINARY_* from .env,
      cloudinary.config({ secure:true, timeout:120000 }).
      makeUpload(folder,{resourceType}) returns an object with
      .single/.array/.fields/.none — SAME call signature as before, so no
      route code changed at the call sites.
    - All 9 upload routes already migrated to makeUpload() and store the
      Cloudinary https URL (req.file.path) instead of /uploads/<f>/<name>:
      products, categories, sliders, influencerReels (image+video,
      resourceType:'auto'), logoSettings, brands, comboBoxSettings,
      aboutSettings/contactSettings, combos (in authRoutes).

  THE BUG WE HIT (and the real fix)
    - Symptom: every admin image upload through the API returned
      {"success":false,"message":"Server error","error":"Request Timeout"}.
    - Ruled out: credentials + connectivity were FINE — cloudinary.api.ping()
      returned status:ok, and direct cloudinary.uploader.upload(path) /
      upload_stream(buffer) both succeeded in ~1-3s from this machine.
    - ROOT CAUSE: version incompatibility. multer is 2.0.2 but
      multer-storage-cloudinary is 4.0.0, which was written for multer 1.x.
      With multer 2.x the file stream never flushes into the storage engine,
      so the Cloudinary stream never gets .end() → hangs until the SDK's
      timeout fires. That is why direct SDK calls worked but the route stalled.
    - FIX: dropped multer-storage-cloudinary entirely (npm uninstall). 
      config/cloudinary.js now uses multer.memoryStorage() to buffer the file,
      then a wrap() middleware pushes each buffer to Cloudinary via
      cloudinary.uploader.upload_stream and sets file.path = secure_url,
      file.filename = public_id (exact shape the routes expect). 100MB limit
      to cover reel videos. On upload error → 500 JSON
      {success:false, message:'Image upload failed'}.

  VERIFIED
    - cloudinary.api.ping() → status ok (creds valid).
    - POST /api/categories with a real image (admin token) → 200,
      image_url = https://res.cloudinary.com/douotprmz/.../cavero/categories/...
      Ran 3x back-to-back, all OK in ~2.7s. Test categories + test Cloudinary
      assets cleaned up afterward.
    - Backend boots clean after removing the package; /api/categories → 200.

  STILL TO DO (deploy phase — unchanged)
    - Deploy: managed Postgres, host backend+frontend, set env vars, DNS for
      caverofragrance.com.
    - Post-deploy: set Razorpay webhook URL + RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET in .env;
      Google OAuth Console (authorized JS origin + consent app name = Cavero);
      GA4 + Search Console.
    - Go-live: flip RAZORPAY_MODE=live, ROTATE all secrets that were shared in
      chat (DB pw, JWT secret, Cloudinary api_secret, Google client secret),
      keep .env gitignored, do a ₹1 live test then refund.
    - Playwright selector-drift pass (separate, non-blocking).


====================================================
  SESSION 47 — PRODUCTION DEPLOY PREP (GitHub + Render + Neon + Cloudinary)
  Last Updated : 2026-06-28
====================================================

  STACK (user-chosen): GitHub → Render (one Node web service) + Neon (Postgres)
  + Cloudinary (media). All-in-one: server.js serves the Angular build
  (dist/cliniva/browser) AND /api, so frontend+backend are SAME-ORIGIN
  (environment.ts apiGatewayBaseUrl=''). No CORS, no separate frontend host.
  Vercel ruled out — can't run the persistent Express server / setInterval
  cleanup / webhooks, and can't host Postgres.

  CODE CHANGES (committed on branch main; repo git-initialized this session)
    - config/db.js — uses DATABASE_URL + SSL when present (Neon); falls back to
      individual DB_* vars (local, no SSL). DB_SSL=true forces SSL on fallback.
    - server.js — CSP widened (helmet): scriptSrc/connectSrc/frameSrc/fontSrc now
      allow Razorpay (checkout+api), Google sign-in+fonts, GA4/gtag, GSAP
      (jsdelivr), lordicon. Only breaks in PROD (ng serve has no CSP; Express
      serves prod frontend with CSP). Reusable arrays RAZORPAY/GOOGLE/ANALYTICS/CDNS.
    - checkout.component.ts — fixed flatMap union-type error the prod build caught
      (typed callback return as OrderItemPayload[]). combo_id pricing logic unchanged.
    - .gitignore — UN-IGNORED src/environments/environment.ts (prod build needs it;
      no secrets inside). Added **/assets/uploads/ (stray backend/src/.../reels
      jpeg had slipped past the root-anchored rule). .env still ignored.
    - render.yaml (NEW, repo root) — Render blueprint. buildCommand:
      "npm install --include=dev && npm run build && npm install --prefix
      backend/auth-service"  (--include=dev REQUIRED: Render sets NODE_ENV=
